- **S340 (2026-09-02) — the E2E suite had been dead for 17 hours because the local preview was never taught the edge's other half.**

  **Every push for over 17 hours failed the E2E Test Suite, eight consecutive runs, and nothing surfaced it.** Both the `e2e` and `compliance` jobs died on the same `HTTP smoke pre-gate`: `scripts/smoke-http.mjs` asserted `/vaultsparked/` and `/ranks/` as `200` carrying the body of stub pages that S335 deleted. Because the smoke is a PRE-gate, its two failures also hid eight further stranded Playwright specs that never got to run.

  **The root cause was an asymmetry S338 fixed around rather than fixed.** `scripts/local-preview-server.mjs` deliberately parses `_headers` — its own comment says "matching what the real CDN sends — keeps synthetic scores representative" — and never parsed `_redirects`. So the preview answers every retired route with 404 where the edge answers 301. That is the identical asymmetry that cost S338 twenty-seven hours of Lighthouse verdicts; S338 corrected the three workflow target lists and left the preview unfaithful. The preview now applies `_redirects` with the edge's precedence (redirects before static assets, as proven live by the S334 `/solara/*` splat), so every stranded consumer resolves at once and every future route merge is covered automatically. (D-S340.1)

  **The smoke now asserts the merge CONTRACT, derived rather than typed.** Consolidated routes are checked for `301` and their exact `Location`, and the list comes from `config/route-consolidation.json`, so a merge is covered the moment it is recorded and can never again be asserted as a page that no longer exists. Coverage went from 12 hand-written checks to **26, zero failures**, and all eight formerly-stranded specs pass (26 passed / 0 failed against the preview). (D-S340.2)

  **The S338 gate for exactly this class stayed green through all 17 hours, and now follows the invocation edge.** `check-workflow-audit-targets.mjs` read absolute URLs and shell loops inside workflow YAML; the offending routes lived one hop in, inside a script the workflow runs by name. It now follows `node scripts/<x>.mjs` into the script and judges the routes it declares, inheriting the job's local-preview provenance. Two refinements were forced by making it real: a target expecting a **3xx** is asserting a contract rather than auditing a page (without which the gate would have refused this session's own repair), and an entry the runner **skips** asserts nothing — which the gate found against itself on its first live run. 29/29, and reproduced in both directions against the real files. (D-S340.3)

  **The postbuild ordering question, carried and deferred twice, is answered by instrument.** `scripts/lib/postbuild-fs-trace.cjs` preloads into each of the 22 steps and observes the real fs calls, so helper indirection is visible where two sessions of static reading were wrong in both directions. A step that writes back the page it read is transforming rather than observing it, and a write reproducing the bytes on disk strands nothing — both distinctions were required before the output meant anything. **S338's fix holds:** `build-news-visual-receipts` runs at #15, after every page writer. (D-S340.4)

  **The instrument then found a defect nobody was hunting.** `propagate-nav.mjs` (#5) strips the `/evidence/` link from the nav AND footer of 125 pages on every build, and `generate-evidence-hub.mjs` (#13) puts it back — confirmed directly on `journal/index.html`, which goes 2 → 0 → 2. Net-zero across a full chain, so `git status` is clean and no surface-vs-surface gate can see it. `/evidence/` was never registered in `config/intelligence-suite.json`, the canonical nav source. **Deliberately not fixed:** that config feeds five consumers, and the blast radius does not belong in a deploy session. Boarded with the reproduction attached. (D-S340.5)

  **Two tests were red behind the pre-gate and both were asserting fossils.** `s103-surfaces` asked a retired alias for four marketing strings that survive nowhere in the tree; it now asserts the merge contract and the tier ladder's shape. `pages.spec` looked for a password form on a login page that delegates to Obelisk (CANON-045); it now asserts the seal, the handoff, and **zero** password inputs — so a local credential form appearing on a delegated surface fails the suite, which the old selector could never have caught. (D-S340.6)

  **Carried with the decision already made:** the cover-artwork duplication is settled by precedent, not taste — D-S339.6 removed the baked status word because baked text goes stale against its feed, and the kicker and title are feed-derived from the same catalog, so the covers go art-only and the tile owns all text. Execution deferred because regenerating every cover rotates the home page's LCP asset and invalidates every cover-bound receipt. (D-S340.7)

- **S339 (2026-09-02) — the origin the release ceremony verifies against had never had a publisher, and three shipped products were being advertised as "still building" on the home page.** The founder authorized a direct push to `main` and a full production deploy.

  **S338 asked "what deploys staging and why did it stop"; the answer was that nothing ever did.** `website.staging.vaultsparkstudios.com` is named 14 times across the workflows and every one of those references READS it — `run-release-ceremony --url=<staging>`, the Lighthouse targets, the uptime probe, the cache purge. The repo's only publisher, `scripts/deploy-staging-content.mjs`, was invoked by zero workflows and reachable only through an npm alias nothing called. Both runs on record were by hand. So CANON-007 had been running backwards: the release ceremony was clearing a tree five days newer than the one it measured, and no probe could see it because every surface agreed with every other surface. `hetzner.ssh` was `READY 2/2` throughout, so under CANON-019 this was agent work rather than a founder blocker — ran the overlay, which promoted **340 files with 25 safe removals**, exact-byte verified through the edge nonce normalisation, identity untouched. The advertised surface went from 23 routes missing to **135/135, zero missing**. `surfaceParity` then graduated from reported to gating: `classifyStatus()` consumes it, an unmeasurable surface holds at yellow rather than passing as matched, and the artifact carries the remedy command. (D-S339.1)

  **The class is now gated, and staging is honestly declared operator-published.** `scripts/check-verification-origin-publisher.mjs` requires every origin a workflow names to be declared in `config/verification-origins.json` with a publisher that exists, actually references the origin, and is reachable by the exact route it claims — an `automated` claim needs a workflow that really invokes it, an `operator` claim needs an npm script that really exists and really runs it. That second half is what catches "reachable only through an alias nothing calls" being presented as a publication path. Run live with the staging declaration removed, the gate reproduces the exact defect and names all five workflows that verify against it. 19/19 self-test. Staging is declared `operator` rather than `automated` because **CI holds no Hetzner SSH credential and is deliberately not given one** — a root key reachable from every workflow run is a blast-radius expansion that belongs to the founder, not to an agent — and the gating flip is what makes drift block a release instead of passing unnoticed. (D-S339.2)

  **The lossy-receipt-reader class moved from a fixed instance to a harness.** S338 closed it for `build-deploy-currency` with an inline fixed point plus a companion proving the guard can fail; that pairing is the entire value, because a fixed point over a function that drops the same field on both passes is self-consistently green. Extracted to `scripts/lib/receipt-roundtrip.mjs` and enforced by `check-receipt-roundtrip-coverage.mjs` (15/15): any script re-deriving from a receipt it wrote itself must import the harness and call the paired form. `build-deploy-currency` refactored onto it, holds 87/87. Audited the whole tree — **exactly one re-derive site exists today**, so the class is closed everywhere it occurs; the gate exists for the second site, which is where all three historical losses happened. The gate's first live run produced a false positive worth keeping as a test: it flagged a script re-deriving from a CONFIG file it only reads, which is not a round trip because there is no emitter to drift from. (D-S339.3)

  **The home page had been lying about three shipped products.** S247 bound each destination page's hero badge to the nav grouping; the home page was a third surface, bound to nothing. PromoGrind sat under the "🔥 Sparked" heading wearing a "⚒️ Forge" badge — contradicting the heading directly above it — while Velaxis and Vorn sat in the FORGE tier entirely, although the catalog, the nav and all three of their own destination pages said SPARKED. Every existing coherence gate was green. Moved Velaxis and Vorn into the Sparked tier and removed the per-card badge from all 11 tier cards, which also closes the S338 doubled-label item — the tier heading states the fact once, so a card repeating it is redundant when it agrees and a lying surface when it does not. Note S338 recorded that badge as client-side rendered and absent from the markup; it was in fact static markup, missed by a case-sensitive search for `SPARKED`. `check-home-portfolio-status-coherence.mjs` (17/17) binds tier placement and badge absence to the canonical feeds, resolving cards by local slug or feed URL. Its own first live run reported "10 cards coherent" on a page with 11 — the sealed-vault teaser is `class="card card-stub"` and an exact-match selector skipped it silently — so the selector now matches the class list and stub exemption is a declared kind. (D-S339.4)

  **Carried honestly:** the `postbuild` ordering audit needs an instrumented run that would perturb the exact build being converged for this session's deploy, so it goes back to the board with the method attached rather than being half-answered from a grep that was demonstrably wrong in both directions. (D-S339.5)

- **S338 (2026-09-02) — three gates were not doing what their names promised, and one of them had been switched off for two sessions.** The founder authorized a direct push to `main` and a full production deploy. The audit found its work in the CI signals rather than in the site.

  **The red `uptime-probe` cron was a symptom, and the board's proposed fix would not have worked.** The board recorded it as a race — the job runs `build-deploy-currency --probe` early, then the seal chain, then `build-deploy-currency --check`, which reported `receipt drifted` — and proposed reordering the `--check`. Reproduced locally instead: the round trip itself is lossy. `observationFromReceipt()` rebuilds the observation from the committed receipt so non-probe runs stay pure, and it restores `retainedForHours` (added S300), `quorum`, `shellParity` and `historyComplete` (added S316) — but never `undeployedContentCommits`, `oldestUndeployedContentAt` or `contentLagHours`, the S336 content clock. The third time a field has been added to the emitter and forgotten in the reader.

  **The consequence was much larger than a red cron.** `classify()` reads `contentLagHours`. So every NON-probe re-derive — `npm run build:check`, the content lane, every local build — collapsed it to null, and the S336 content ceiling, built specifically to catch the S335 shape where a whole release sits unserved in production, had been **silently disabled since the session that created it**. A gate that cannot fire is indistinguishable from a gate that passes. The three fields are restored; more importantly the class is closed by a fixed-point self-test that names no field at all — for a fully-populated observation, `derive(read(derive(x))) === derive(x)` — with a companion case proving the guard can actually fail, because a fixed-point test over a function that drops the same field on both passes is self-consistently green. Proven in both directions: reverting the fix fails the new case naming all three fields; restoring it passes 87/87 (was 85), and the exact CI sequence `--probe` then `--check` exits 0 with `contentLagHours: 1.3` intact. An S300 test of precisely this shape already existed and stayed green throughout the defect window, because its three fixtures omitted the fields that were being dropped. (D-S338.1)

  **Lighthouse CI had been red for 15 consecutive runs across ~27 hours, and no surface said so.** Every run since 2026-09-01 04:12Z failed with `ERRORED_DOCUMENT_REQUEST` on `http://127.0.0.1:4173/ranks/`. `/ranks/` was consolidated into `/leaderboards/#ranks` and its page deleted; `_redirects` answers it with a 301 at the edge. But the Lighthouse job audits the **local preview server**, which serves the built tree and applies no `_redirects`, no Worker routes and no Pages rules — so the redirect that makes production correct cannot apply there, and the audit asked for a page that cannot exist. One 404 fails the whole job, so the site's performance gate produced no verdict for over a day. `/vaultsparked/` was in the same state on the staging list, audited alongside the `/membership/` it redirects to, and both routes still carried entries in `config/lighthouse-route-tiers.json`.

  Removed from all four consumers, and the class gated: `scripts/check-workflow-audit-targets.mjs` runs in `build:check` with two rules that name no route — no audit target may be a `from` in `config/route-consolidation.json`, and every local-preview target must resolve to a real page in the git-tracked tree. Both derive from config that already exists, so the next route merge protects itself the moment it is recorded. Proven by restoring the pre-fix state: exit 1 naming `/ranks/` and its replacement, exit 0 once cleared. 18/18 self-test; live, 11 distinct route targets against 16 guarded consolidated routes. The gate also caught its own first-run defect — a shell warmup loop ends `.../chaperone/; do`, so the last token arrived with the command separator attached and a real audited page read as a phantom missing one. (D-S338.2)

  **Staging parity became a number instead of an anecdote.** `check-staging-parity.mjs` compared a hand-maintained sample of three routes — `/`, `/studio-pulse/`, `/membership/` — which is enough to compare shells, headers and CSP, and structurally incapable of noticing that a route production serves is simply absent from staging. That is why S337 could only record `/how-we-build/` 404-vs-200 as prose. Growing the hand list would strand the next new route identically, so parity is now measured over the surface each origin *advertises*: two sitemap GETs, complete coverage, and a new route covered the moment it enters the sitemap. Live: **production advertises 134 routes, staging 115, 23 missing on staging** — `/evidence/`, `/how-we-build/`, three news editions and the entire `.ai/` fact-sheet layer — and staging's `api/build-sha.json` reports `94e78e93` built **2026-08-28**, five days behind production.

  It is published as `surfaceParity` with `gating: false` and the reason stated on the artifact. Wiring it into `classifyStatus()` today would redden `staging-health` and block releases on a blocker already on the board that nobody has fixed — measure before gating. The probe's own first live run demonstrated the same discipline in miniature: staging's sitemap names the canonical **production** origin, so an origin-filtered read returned 0 of 115 entries, and `compareSurfaces()` reported `uncomparable` rather than inventing a clean parity out of an unreadable list. (D-S338.3)

- **S337 (2026-09-02) — the full deploy was never identity-blocked, and what actually blocked it was a test that could only be right in one browser.** The founder authorized a direct push and a full production deploy. Three surfaces — the S336 board item and two `PROJECT_STATUS.json` blockers — recorded that deploy as gated on the Obelisk identity hold. Probed before acting: `check-promotion-scope --check` returns `promotable=true · scoped-disjoint`, and under real dispatch conditions the gate returns `allowed=true; mode=scoped`. That has been true since S319, when the blast-radius resolver landed. The hold is not cleared and `auth/**`, `surface:identity` and `worker:identity` stay held and named on the public receipt — but a candidate disjoint from that radius may promote, and this one is. Eighteen sessions of prose had been asserting a blocker that the gate itself disagreed with.

  **The dispatched deploy then failed 9/10 on the release ceremony, and the cause was ours but not the site's.** `staging-browser-receipt` rejected with reason `flaky-1`: the Firefox run of "mobile drawer and every theme are readable" failed and its retry passed. `tests/staging-release.spec.js` deliberately classifies Trusted Types Report-Only console notices as observations rather than errors, because `require-trusted-types-for 'script'` ships Report-Only by design while the soak runs — but it recognised only **Chromium's** phrasing of that notice. Firefox words it entirely differently, so in Firefox every report-only notice fell through to `consoleErrors` and failed `expect(consoleErrors).toEqual([])`. The sinks involved render asynchronously, so it fired on some runs and not others, which Playwright calls flaky, and a flaky result rejects the ceremony. A correct site and a correct security posture, stopped by a single-engine assertion wearing a three-engine test matrix.

  Fixed conjunctively — a report-only marker **and** a Trusted Types marker — so classification is engine-agnostic without widening; an ENFORCED violation carries no report-only marker and still fails loudly, which is the signal the enforce flip depends on. The classifier moved to `tests/lib/tt-report-only.js` (a spec importing another spec double-registers its tests) and is pinned by a regression spec carrying each engine's verbatim string, curly quotation marks included, copied from the receipt of the run that blocked the deploy. **Proven: 6/6 staging release tests pass locally across chromium, firefox and webkit, zero flake.**

  **The real Trusted Types blocker was measured while there, and deliberately not fixed.** `ambient-core.bundle.js` installs the TT `default` policy the site's ~167 legacy `innerHTML` sinks depend on, and its own comment says it "MUST load before any sink usage" — it is first *within* ambient-core, but ambient-core is not the first script on the page. Across 137 built pages, **31 sink-bearing client assets load before it**, led by `pwa-nav.js` on 81 pages and `pwa-install.js` on 72. Report-Only hides this; enforcement throws. That is a far more actionable blocker than the "stale soak evidence" the board records, and it is on the board with its numbers — the repair rewrites the head of every page and invalidates every hash-bound receipt at once, which is a session, not a rider on a deploy. The one asset that actually fired (`stats-surface.js`, a static scaffold with no interpolation) was converted to DOM calls and is no longer a sink.

  **Two Desk truth defects closed at the source.** `factCandidates` scored register and never subject, so the 2026-08-31 edition published a syndicated vacuum-cleaner promo block as its first sourced fact under a real publisher URL; a relevance term now penalises sentences sharing no significant token with the story, reusing the existing `titleTokens`/`tokenOverlap` helpers, and is disabled when no topic is supplied. And `authorDraft` was discarding the `model` and `fellBackFrom` that `chat()` sets specifically so a caller can disclose a standby author — so no published story recorded who wrote it, and with the preferred model depooled the `/news/` disclosure was stating an assumption. Stories now carry an `authoredBy` receipt separating what was requested from what answered. Separately, `news-publish.yml` ran the radar as `--scan || echo "…"`, making a crash and an empty queue the same green step; the scan now emits its own verdict and counts.

- **S336 (2026-09-02) — the deploy path had been broken since S334, and an entire session's release had never reached a reader.** The founder asked for an arc ending in a full deploy. The deploy was impossible. `prune-served-surface.mjs` deletes every file not positively classified by `config/served-surface.json` and then refuses if the prune broke a route the site advertises — and `/evidence/` (added S334) and `/how-we-build/` (added S335) had never been added to that hand-maintained allowlist. Both pages exist, are linked, and are listed in `sitemap.xml`. Every deploy path, content lane and full production alike, refused.

  The failure is delayed and **self-planting**, which is why two sessions shipped straight past it: the content lane promotes `sitemap.xml`, so a newly-added route only becomes *advertised in production* on one deploy and only breaks the **next** one. S334 added `/evidence/` and armed the trap for S335; S335 added `/how-we-build/` and armed it for S336. Meanwhile production was still serving content-lane head `d858e0a4` from 2026-09-01 15:50Z — eight hours before the S335 feat commit. The member-write privilege lockdown, Season 1, the community wall, the dashboard quota meter and `/how-we-build/` were all in `main` and none of them were being served. `/how-we-build/` returned 404 to the public. S335's own handoff had recorded "content lane deploys on push"; `pages-deploy.yml` states the opposite — pushes and schedules evaluate the interlock but **cannot deploy**, and promotion is manual-dispatch only.

  Fixed, promoted and verified: `/how-we-build/` now returns **200**, the served `api/build-sha.json` reports `contentLaneHead 88393a29` (which contains `aff64499`), 194 paths promoted, and `deploy-currency` reads `content-current · shell parity matched`. `vault-wall/` was deliberately *restored* to the manifest rather than left removed: the content lane cannot delete files or promote `_redirects`, so production genuinely still serves that page and the deployed sitemap still advertises it — dropping the prefix would make an overlay deploy prune a live advertised route out of the tree it is about to publish. Declaring it an `edgeRoutes` entry was rejected because that asserts a 301 that does not serve today.

  **The gate that should have caught it had never run against real data.** `build:check` invoked `prune-served-surface --self-test`, which exercises pure functions over synthetic fixtures; the only invocation touching the real manifest lived inside the deploy workflow. `--check` now runs the real manifest against the real git-tracked tree and asks whether every advertised route survives its own prune. It was proven by restoring the S335-era manifest: exit 1 naming exactly `/evidence/` and `/how-we-build/`, exit 0 once fixed.

  **And the alarm that should have noticed was watching a clock automation keeps rewinding.** `build-deploy-currency.mjs` measured one quantity — the span from the deployed commit to the repo tip — against a 48h ceiling. Hourly `[skip ci]` publishers commit several times an hour, and a promotion lands on whatever HEAD is at dispatch time, which is nearly always one of those cron commits, so that span never grows. The live reading during the incident was 34 commits behind, `ageHours` 10.1, verdict `behind` — a **pass**. Thirty-four uptime crons and one stranded release are indistinguishable to a commit counter. The receipt now carries a second, independent clock — `undeployedContentCommits`, `oldestUndeployedContentAt`, `contentLagHours` — aged from the **oldest** undeployed hand-authored commit so one fresh commit cannot mask days of waiting behind it, against a much tighter 12h ceiling. "Hand-authored" is decided structurally, never by commit subject or author: a path counts when the served-surface manifest classifies it as served **and** `config/evidence-graph.json` does not declare it a generated output. Content lag is measured against the promoted `contentLaneHead` rather than the deliberately-held baseline sha, so pages a reader can already load are never counted as undeployed, and matched shell parity still returns `content-current` first so the held identity backlog can never cry wolf.

  **`api/tt-readiness.json` was publishing a fresh date over two-month-old evidence.** The artifact is `publicSafe` and gates the Trusted Types enforce flip. It computed no age at all: `amber-soak` held whenever a warm row existed, at any age, forever, while `nextAction` instructed the reader to "wait for warm rows to age out" — nothing aged anything out. It also re-stamped `generatedAt` on every build over a manifest generated **2026-07-07** against its own declared 30-day window. D-S335.7 had called the ageing logic "suspect"; it did not exist. It now computes real ages, publishes `manifestGeneratedAt`/`manifestAgeDays`/`soakWindowDays`/`evidenceStale`, and adds a `stale-evidence` status that keeps `enforceEligible` **false**. That refusal is the load-bearing part: all 17 warm rows report their sinks already gone and would age out, which would have produced `enforce-candidate` out of a fossil. Trusted Types was **not** flipped — the founder's S335 approval stands, but the evidence to act on it does not exist until a fresh KV soak runs.

  Two PostgREST queries on `/community/` were also found to be unparseable: `?eq.is_active=true` had operator and column swapped (probed live: `HTTP 400 PGRST100 "failed to parse filter"`), and the `game_sessions` count filtered `created_at`, a column that table does not have. Recorded honestly as a capability fix rather than a lit surface — the corrected poll query returns `[]` because no poll is currently active, so today's pixels are unchanged; what changed is that the feature can now work at all.

  **Deliberately not fixed:** four more public tables — `challenge_submissions`, `game_sessions`, `point_events`, `member_achievements` — have no anonymous read path, so `/community/`, all seven `/leaderboards/*` and public member profiles render zeros and empty states to signed-out visitors. This is the same class S335 found on `vault_members`, and the remedy generalizes cleanly (definer projection views honouring `public_profile`). It was escalated rather than applied because it decides *which member activity becomes publicly readable*, which is a founder privacy and product decision, not a bug fix. The diagnosis, call sites and proposed shape are in D-S336.5. Real row counts behind those tables were not measured — the sandbox classifier blocked the credentialed probe and it was not worked around — so the finding rests on policy reading, which proves anon can never see a row without quantifying how many are hidden.

- **S335 (2026-09-01) — a live privilege escalation in the member portal closed, a months-old blank spot on every public member surface lit, and the audit's own "shipped" deletion made real.** The session re-ran the full-site audit one day after S334 and found that the highest-value work was not more IA polish. **Any signed-in member could write their own `points`, `plan_key` and `is_sparked`** from the browser: the base policy granted UPDATE on every column with no WITH CHECK, and the live database still carried Supabase's default table grants. `ask-ignis` reads those columns for the paid tier. Phase 61 (`supabase-phase61-member-write-lockdown.sql`) revokes table-wide writes, re-grants UPDATE per profile column, moves gifting into an atomic `gift_points()` RPC, and hardens `purchase_treasury_item()`; it was applied through a new agent-path applier (`scripts/apply-supabase-migration.mjs`, pre-image + post-image + a 9/9 behavioural probe under `set local role`).

  The probe surfaced the second finding: **the members table has no anonymous read policy at all**, so every anonymous public surface — member counts, recently joined, leaderboards, the directory, public profiles, the investor KPI tile — had been rendering empty, and the "opt-out" was a client filter on data nobody could read. A security-definer `public_leaderboard` view (opt-out enforced server-side) is now the public projection, and every anon reader was repointed to it. The public member surfaces show real numbers for the first time.

  **S334's stub deletion had silently reverted**: `build-route-consolidation.mjs` ran in prebuild and postbuild and re-rendered 11 meta-refresh stubs every build; the "settle derived artifacts" commit brought them back. The script is now a court (edge rule present, no stub, no meta-refresh anywhere); 13 stubs are deleted for good; the footer's four retired links point at live anchors. The build chain lost 16 duplicate or dead invocations, `DERIVED_BUILD_PROFILES.full` gained `build-public-status`, early-hints now runs after the final shell rotation, `run-build-check --quiet` exists, ~360 MB of per-session screenshot snapshots left the index, and 69 old audit docs moved to `docs/archive/audits/`.

  **IA:** `/proof/` folded into `/evidence/#verify` (the in-browser verifier moved into the hub generator); `/feedback/` + `/feedback/insights/` into `/changelog/#requests`; `/vault-wall/` into `/community/#wall` (season countdown, rival, rank distribution, podium — its leaderboard had been querying two columns that do not exist). All four are edge 301s with merge analyses in DECISIONS (D-S335.3–5). **New page `/how-we-build/`** explains sessions, SIL, canon, receipts, handoff and the agent surfaces in visitor voice, and a vocabulary gate now fails any public page that uses those operator terms without linking to it. **Season 1 — Ignition** is live in `data/seasons.json` (2026-09-02 → 2026-10-14, Vault Points only); the community events strip lost three expired cards and gained a freshness rule that fails on a past month/quarter label. **Member dashboard:** an IGNIS quota meter (limits from the edge function, live probe for the Sparked cap), one upsell instead of two stacked paywalls (Sparked members get a Desk digest), and a "your feedback that shipped" strip in the Chronicle. **Worker:** Trusted Types enforcement is a one-variable flip (`TT_ENFORCE_ENABLED`, default off) — held because `api/tt-readiness.json` says not eligible (D-S335.7). **CI:** `linkcheck` + `member-seo` merged into `weekly-maintenance.yml`.

  **Disproved by reading the code:** prompt caching on `semantic-search` (prompt below the cacheable minimum) and AVIF conversion of the Desk art masters (served derivatives already optimized). **Carried with diagnosis:** the uptime probe's 48 daily `[skip ci]` commits (Worker-cron design on the board), 47 files that churn between two identical builds (commit-derived feeds, not timestamps), and a `page_feedback` schema drift between the portal writer and the migration.

- **S334 (2026-09-01) — a full-site audit, and two of its own findings retired by measurement.** A 14-item audit over all 185 pages shipped 12 items and **disproved 2**, which is the part worth stating first. The audit claimed the homepage shipped "66KB of critical CSS"; the shared critical-shell block is 5,363 bytes and correctly scoped, the rest is page-unique CSS (only 7% of the homepage's 246 inline selectors also appear in the 192KB shared sheet), and measured mobile LCP is ~900ms with it inline — extracting it would trade a parse for a round trip on the site's fastest page. It claimed the member dashboard's 133KB nine-pane document was a perf defect; it measures FCP 860ms with 1,414 DOM nodes. Chasing the first item surfaced something that looked far worse — `/games/` at 4,724ms FCP against ~1s everywhere else — and a controlled A/B (same harness, alternating variants, stylesheet strategy the only difference) put blocking at 724ms and async at 752ms. The 4,724ms was the first page load in a fresh Chromium process. **S275's blocking-CSS decision stands on its field data; the harness now warms the browser before it believes a number.**

  What did ship. **`_redirects` was 301ing real content into 404s**: `/solara/*` and `/franchise-architect/*` splat rules promised counterparts under the destination that did not exist, so three Solara world pages, the legacy FA build, the Solara SPA bundle and a 30-file app tree all resolved to nothing. A splat is only safe when the destination mirrors the source; retired ROUTES are now listed one by one and the app trees serve themselves. The three Solara world pages (Season Chronicle, Archive of the Fallen, Sun Observatory) were an island — linked only to each other, reachable from nothing — and now sit under `/games/solara/` with their first inbound link. **`/ignis-health/` titled itself "(internal)" and was protected only by robots.txt**, which is a request to polite crawlers, not access control; it is now in the worker's `GATED_PATH_PATTERNS`, and a new court asserts every wildcard-Disallowed path is either gated or explicitly declared public-but-unindexed. Sixteen retired routes shipped a meta-refresh page AND an edge 301 for the same path; the stubs are gone and three routes that had no edge rule got one. `sitemap.html` — a tracked, deployed, byte-drifted duplicate of `/sitemap-page/` with zero inbound links — had been **allowlisted in the orphan gate rather than removed**; deleted, with both exemptions.

  Two fixes were joins, not builds. **`data/pathways.json` has carried a 4-step route per pathway since S201 and the generator threw it away**, so six pages shipped 23KB of chrome around ~530 bytes of headline. The audit proposed deleting them; rendering what was already in the source of truth was better, and four stale step targets pointing at long-retired routes were repointed. Main content per page went 534 → 1,880 bytes. **The 17 `.ai/` fact sheets — index-follow, self-describing, "cite this page" — were referenced by nothing**: not sitemap.xml, not agents.json, not one human page, and carrying no structured data. Root cause for the sitemap absence was one line: `generate-sitemap`'s blanket `startsWith('.')` rule, meant for `.git` and `.cache`, swallowed `.ai` too. Three generator-side joins later they are in the sitemap (18 entries), on 17 agents.json entries, carrying JSON-LD, and cross-linked from every human page — and grounding the answer engine in them took its index from 41 to 58 documents, so **15 of 17 project questions now reach the studio's own canonical sheet**.

  **The Desk now remembers what it published.** `selectDraftableTopic()` held dead-host memory (the S333 fix) and nothing about its own output, so a story holding the top queue slot got drafted three mornings running — 2026-08-21, -22 and -23 all carry `from-atari-to-eve-online-building-on-15-years`. The duplicates were noindexed downstream, so search was never damaged, which is also why it went unnoticed: each still spent an LLM draft, an OG render, and one of four daily publish slots. A repeat carrying a NEW primary source is allowed and logged as a follow-up, because refusing a developing story would be worse than the bug. Verified by replaying the entire published history: **14 legitimate stories allowed, exactly the 2 known duplicates refused, no false positives.**

  **New surface: `/evidence/`.** The site publishes eight live-data pages that all answer versions of "is this studio real and working?" with no front door. Four lanes, each a question a visitor actually arrives with, routing to the deep page that still owns the answer — nothing moved, so no permalink or receipt broke. Freshness is fetched live rather than baked, because a baked stamp ages into a lie; a feed that will not parse reads "freshness unavailable", never green. Ten more pages across the membership and editorial clusters gained a one-line orientation strip saying what they are and which sibling answers the other thing — orientation, not the merge the audit proposed, because each page earns its URL and the real failure was that a visitor picking the wrong door could not tell.

  **Two unrelated gates aged into failure at midnight.** `check-cta-readiness`'s fixtures asserted window-bound wording that only appears while evidence is fresh, and their `asOf` turned 31 days old on 2026-09-01; the function already accepted an injectable clock, the fixtures simply were not passing it. Pinned. `startup-revenue-agreement` remains open: the promoted v5 startup brief renders a revenue age the shared resolver disagrees with, which is a studio-ops brief-renderer concern, not site code — reported rather than masked.

- **S333 (2026-08-30) — The Desk publishes again: a five-day editorial outage traced to a selection bug, not a source drought; plus the gate that was supposed to catch the last one had never run.** `The Desk — Scheduled Publish` had failed **eight consecutive runs** since 2026-08-29, leaving the public newsroom five days stale while the cadence gate honestly reported the staleness it could not fix. Root cause was one line of selection logic: `news-draft-edition.mjs` took only `draftable[0]`, and `draftableTopics()` is a **static** filter (is this URL an aggregator?) that cannot know a publisher URL will answer 401 behind a paywall. One dead Reuters link therefore dropped the whole slot while six readable topics sat in the same queue. Selection now walks the ranked queue until a topic yields real prose, and — because blocking is a property of the **host**, not the story — remembers which hosts refused it, so four consecutive `openai.com` stories cost one attempt instead of the entire budget. Proven live: one 403 spent one attempt, five same-host topics skipped free, `huggingface.co` reached, edition drafted with 3 sourced facts. **The second finding is the sharper one:** S332 "locked the contract with a 24/24 self-test" for exactly this class of bug — but `scripts/lib/build-order.mjs --self-test` appears in **no** npm script and **no** workflow, so that lock had never executed. It is now step 371 of `build:check`, and its hardcoded single-script assertion is joined by a structural one: `invocation-modes.mjs` derives mode-requirement from each producer's own dispatch source (a chain with no default action, whose terminal `else` prints usage and exits nonzero), then requires every profile running such a script to declare a mode it actually recognises — proven to fire on both a reintroduced S332 regression and a typo'd flag. **Evidence honesty:** canonical destination unknowns now carry a consecutive-unknown streak and a last-known-good age, so an `unknown` is readable as a duration rather than a snapshot; the validator forbids a streak from ever promoting a verdict, and the two never-confirmed destinations report `null` rather than a fabricated past. The Google News aggregator token was investigated and **rejected** as a source: the modern `AU_yqL…` format embeds no publisher URL and exposes no redirect or canonical, so resolving it would require Google's undocumented `batchexecute` RPC — not a dependency worth putting under a public editorial engine. The desk's honest `VaultSparkNewsDesk/1.0` user-agent was likewise kept rather than spoofed to evade publisher bot policy. **A fifth finding surfaced during closeout verification:** the public forge ledger (`api/commit-map.json`) was publishing **zero** entries. Its noise filter was working exactly as designed — it just had nothing but noise to read, because the ledger scanned a fixed last-120 commits and 128 consecutive `[skip ci]` publisher commits had accumulated since the S332 closeout, burying every human commit below the window. A scan window sized by a raw commit count is outrun by any cron that commits faster than humans do; depth is now sized to the 24 entries the ledger actually displays, with an early exit so a normal run costs the same. Restored live: 0 → 24 entries. **A seventh defect surfaced only after deploying:** with selection fixed, the Desk got as far as preparing a real draft in production CI (2/2 sources, 6 sourced facts) and then failed at authoring — Hetzner had **retired the pinned model** `Qwen/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-FP8` while still advertising it in `GET /models`, answering 503 on every completion while `Qwen3.8-27B` answered 200 on the same credentials. The publisher behaved correctly throughout, refusing to ship a half-written edition. The Desk now declares an ordered standby and discloses which model authored via `fellBackFrom`; failover is narrow by design, and a 429 quota fact is never retried against another model. **Outcome, verified at the served surface:** the Desk published edition `2026-08-31` — its first successful run after nine consecutive failures — and it is live at https://vaultsparkstudios.com/news/2026-08-31/inside-metas-push-to-put-robots-to-work-in/ with the public freshness feed reading `daily · age 0d`, down from `periodic · age 6d`. Two independent defects had to be fixed in sequence for a single edition to exist, and the second was invisible until the first was repaired. **Three review cycles then found five more defects in the session's own work**, which is the part worth remembering: a failover loop shipped with its predicate tested and the loop itself uncovered; a second live instance of the fixed-window burial in `build-ignis-conduit` hiding 84% of real activity; a `publishedSlugs({windowDays:0})` boundary bug that could only surface on a day the Desk had published and did, the same day, unnoticed because its self-test was the **third** orphaned test found this session and the only one actively failing; and a receipt-binding diagnosis this session got wrong twice in opposite directions before measuring 24 commits settled it. Both escalated decisions were then delegated and resolved: proof receipts now bind the promotion candidate rather than cron churn (false invalidation gone, tamper detection proven intact), and the Desk corroborates across outlets at a stricter-than-merge bar rather than loosening clustering — rejecting the loosening as a truth risk and tuning the alternative on live data (0.55 changed nothing; 0.45 doubled the publishable pool).

- **S329 (2026-08-24) — Mega-audit sprint, phases 1–3 of 8: sitewide truth drift closed, Desk slug reruns made unshippable, and the micro-feedback loop finally transmits.** A three-agent full-site audit (page inventory/redundancy · member/AI/engagement · build/perf/security) produced an 8-phase founder-approved plan; phases 1–3 landed in one session across three verified pushes. **Truth:** the footer's "27 initiatives" (byte-duplicated on 125 pages) and both builder literals now derive from `api/public-intelligence.json → portfolio.total`, and `check-press-kit-drift` sweeps every git-tracked banner carrier (125 pages, proven-fail on a doctored deep page); the internal traffic-light health grade no longer publishes to the AI corpus; six root-junk files are gone; CANON-053's adoption row cites the real verifier. **Editorial:** the Desk published the same slug three consecutive days (2026-08-21..23) because every dedupe layer compared AI-rewritten headlines — `scoreTopic` now hard-blocks published-slug matches, `--promote` refuses cross-date reruns, the 8/22+8/23 copies are consolidated (`supersededBy` → the 8/21 canonical; noindex, honest superseded banner, out of the index listing), and the new `check-news-slug-uniqueness` gate (proven-fail on the live dup) locks the class. Registry truth: game-registry 8→11 (voidfall, vaultspark-forge); Scriptorium's page stopped claiming "Forging" (sparked variant — the tool is live behind auth); Franchise Architect's shard rejoined llms.txt/agents.json via a shared ROUTE_ALIAS; Call of Doodie's forge/SPARKED drift is sibling-owned and shipped as an Ark repo-question; `api/ignis-roi.json` unfroze (its `generatedAt` was a hardcoded literal — now evidence-derived) and joined the build chain + trust-feed ceilings. **Feedback:** the sitewide micro-feedback survey wrote localStorage and stopped for 100+ sessions — the anonymous usefulness enum now inserts into `page_feedback` (privacy-honest copy, e2e POST test with capability-keyed skip, supabase-client added to 5 mount pages); the vault-member Connected Games panel stopped promising auto-flowing stats (Games Tracked 5→0 honest); the feedback-sentiment cron was correctly routed to studio-ops via Ark (its contract forbids service-role work here). Verified per push: build:check 368→370/370 · mobile runtime 235/235 · theme-matrix receipts + changed-surface captures inspected. **Open and named:** phases 4–8 (IA consolidation, security/Turnstile//ask-founders/, vault-narrative→free inference + slim cron build + journal revival, perf, elite features) are the next-session runway; `propagate-nav.mjs`'s hand-arrays are stale vs live pages (clobbered 126 pages when run — reverted; reconcile before any future run); Cloudflare Web Analytics still doesn't observe the production hostname (founder console unlock — starves every reaction/RUM floor).

- **S328 (2026-08-24) — The publish-cascade gate can now see `.cache/`, and the CTA readiness surface states its real bar.** The 4-hourly `refresh-live-data` cron regenerated both `api/funnel-summary.json` and the `.cache/cta-readiness.json` derived from it, but staged only `api/` — committing the producer and stranding the byte-checked consumer every cycle. Because the commit carries `[skip ci]`, no CI run ever observed it, and `build:check` went red at step 57/368 on the next clean tree. The cron now stages the consumer. More consequentially, `check-publish-cascade-coverage` — the gate written to prevent exactly this — **passed on the run that shipped it and could never have failed**, because it derives its universe from `config/evidence-graph.json`, which held 33 nodes and zero under `.cache/`. That directory is no longer excluded: `.cache/cta-readiness.json` is the graph's first `.cache/` node, and removing the staging line now makes the gate exit 1 naming the workflow and artifact. Separately, `check-cta-readiness` no longer promises a cumulative post-epoch total it never measured — `counts.shown` is a rolling 30-day count, so the row now carries `basis`, `windowDays`, and `observedThrough`, states the requirement as *within a single 30-day window*, and reports a distinct no-post-epoch-span verdict rather than a confident countdown over frozen evidence; `minShown`, `WINDOW_DAYS`, and the epoch are deliberately unchanged. A genius-list suppressor pinned to a stale epoch literal (the value a sibling gate's self-test defines as *wrong*) now reads the shared contract registry. **Open and named:** 17 other byte-checked `--check` gates touch `.cache/` and remain undeclared in the graph.

- **S327 (2026-08-23) — The Desk satire system is clear, collision-free, immutable by default, and live.** Article art now has one text authority: text-free source imagery is composited into an opaque masthead and caption safe zone, while generated pages no longer repeat the punchline beneath the image or leak visual-description prose into satire copy. Scene/alt parity and meta-caption rejection fail closed. The August 21–23 artwork was rebuilt from reviewed text-free ImageGen masters; the newest line is “Fifteen years later, the tutorial level is an open world.” A 28-capture matrix covering the News index and newest article across seven themes at desktop/mobile was manually reviewed with zero defects, and production desktop/mobile captures confirm no overlapping or conflicting text. Scheduled `--rebuild` now preserves every complete reviewed PNG/WebP/AVIF family, fails on partial families, and requires explicit `--refresh-art` to overwrite; the publisher workflow rejects tracked art mutation. Exact staging and production verification cover 12 pages, 36 assets, the newest 2026-08-23 route, and five claim rows. Content-only production run 32662840244 completed green; the full-site/identity hold remains untouched. Canonical build/check is 368/368, exact E2E/compliance/accessibility/mobile/Lighthouse CI is green, and the live `/status/` Desk tile reports the August 23 run.

- **S326 (2026-08-22) — The Desk recovery is fully deployed and independently verified in production.** The scheduled newsroom published two August 21 editions and one August 22 edition after the August 11 gap. Every live article now shows estimated read time plus privacy-thresholded Reader views; thin traffic honestly reads `Collecting`, and `/v/desk-presence` answers 204. A post-deploy audit caught one secondary inconsistency the release workflow had missed: `api/news-desk-claims.ndjson` was generated and committed but withheld by the content lane because only `api/*.json` was promotable. The gate now allows that one canonical public NDJSON path by exact name while every other NDJSON path remains blocked. The exception passed 43/43 hotfix-gate and 63/63 content-lane self-tests, staging served five August 22 records, canonical build/check passed 368/368, exact commit CI passed E2E/compliance/mobile/accessibility/local+staging Lighthouse, and production deployment run 32605433768 promoted 137 paths. Live proof: all new article routes 200, freshness daily through August 22, feed ordered through August 22, five August 22 claim rows, visible `~1 min`/`Reader views`/`Collecting`, and content-lane receipt head `ef703658c814d913c5ed4b553fcd787c64ee3777`.

- **S325 (2026-08-21) — The Desk is publishing again, and every article now carries truthful reader statistics.** The scheduled publisher had not produced a real edition since August 11 because its workflow invoked the radar without the required scan mode, skipped dependency installation, discarded authored body/visual fields, and allowed stale cadence to pass. The workflow now runs the real scan, installs its locked dependencies, authors with the configured free self-hosted inference path, generates and pixel-checks article-bound art before promotion, rebuilds engagement/reaction/stats/pages in dependency order, and fails if no current daily edition exists. A new source-bound edition, **“Memory Configs: The +9.5% Lift That Actually Works,”** is in the corpus for August 21 with bespoke art and full article copy. Every article renders estimated read time and privacy-thresholded reader views above the fold and in its evidence panel; measured engaged time replaces the estimate only when real observations qualify, while sub-floor traffic honestly reads “Collecting.” The broader S325 arc also shipped the multi-family 233/233 verification-reachability ratchet, a cryptographic closed-day velocity proof, and the public four-hour promotion-cadence contract. Verification: canonical build/check **368/368**, News coherence **12/12 + 8 live panels**, manually reviewed visual matrix **28/28** across seven themes and desktop/mobile, mobile runtime **235/235** with zero P0/P1, and refreshed Linux visual baselines from successful Actions run 32446357122.

- **S323 (2026-08-19) — The gate-name honesty sweep found a class, not two instances: ten CI gates were reading as clean passes on a property their body never measured.** S321 (D-S321.4) found `check-public-note-freshness` measured voice regexes under a "freshness" name and left a standing item warning "there is no reason to believe they are the only two." This session ran that dedicated sweep across all 173 `check-*.mjs` gates (five in-process reader agents, no OS windows), verified every candidate against live code, and fixed ten offenders — each to measure the property its name promises, each locked with a self-test that fails in the direction the old gate never could. The most serious was structural and security-relevant: **`check-worker-rewriter-safety` defined and self-tested four unsafe-op scanners but `runScan()` composed only two** — the nonce-branch `Content-Type`-drop and the HEAD-request cache-poison guards were dead against the real Worker while green in self-test (the self-test calls the scanners directly). All four now flow through one exported `scanWorkerSafety`, with a composition self-test that fails if any registered scanner stops running live; the Worker was read before the gate was touched and confirmed safe on all four (wiring the HEAD scanner surfaced that its regex had drifted from the Worker's *strengthened* GET guard, not a Worker bug). Also fixed: `check-canon-compliance` (CANON-008 passed on a canon-id substring the propagated index always carries → now requires a real license artifact); `check-launch-ready` (case-sensitive `'SPARKED'` vs the registry's lowercase `'sparked'` silently disabled all SPARKED enforcement, and it read `liveUrl` where the field is `runtimeUrl` → both fixed, repo now 100% GO); `check-news-engagement-coherence` (engaged-time drift, not just fabrication); `check-build-step-resilience` (unguarded gitignored reads across 82 build scripts); `check-game-playability-coherence` (sourceRepo check ran only on already-flagged pages); `check-registry-freshness` (a `urlDrift` bucket declared, returned, never filled → surfaced a real mindframe drift); `check-hero-jsonld-completeness` (empty arrays counted as present); `check-journal-dates` (comma ≠ day); `check-portfolio-coherence` (a sitemap leg advertised in the header but never read). `build:check` 319/319, confirmed by a real captured exit code — the first run's background wrapper reported exit 0 while the command's own exit was 1. Surfaced advisories held for their owners (CANON-018): `mindframe` registry URL drift, `franchise-architect` portfolio drift, and the registry storing the live URL under `runtimeUrl` while `liveUrl` is sparse. No served HTML/JS/Worker code changed; the resynced derived feeds reflect the pulled hourly-cron churn.
- **S322 (2026-08-19) — Route-provenance corroboration is now CI-reachable, and a small S321 write-back gap is closed.** Session opened with cut-off triage (write-back-currency probe, F7): a commit landed 15h after S321's closeout — wiring `verify-provider-chain.mjs` and `check-public-note-freshness` self-tests into `check-proof-surface` after `check-orphan-scripts` correctly flagged the new script as referenced by nothing — but the closure was never recorded (D-S321.3). The code was already shipped and pushed, so this session documented it rather than redoing it. **Main work:** carried item from S321 (`[S321][SIL][RELEASE/P1]`) — production is bot-challenged for datacenter clients, so `api/worker-route-provenance.json`'s primary receipt can only ever come from a locally-run probe, and content promotion has depended on that manual step ever since (unchanged; still a blocker). `scripts/build-worker-route-provenance.mjs --probe` now *also* probes the staging `workers_dev` origin — unchallenged, CI-reachable — and writes an additive `buildVantage` field (`attests: "build"`, its own `state`/`summary`/`sourceSha256`) alongside the untouched primary receipt. CI's existing `uptime-probe.yml` `--probe` step picks this up with zero workflow changes, so every hourly cron now corroborates that the current build is route-correct even while production stays vantage-challenged in CI. The distinction is load-bearing and enforced structurally, not just by convention: `check-content-capability-slice.mjs` still requires `receipt.observedOrigin === 'https://vaultsparkstudios.com'` verbatim and reads only the untouched `routes[]` array, so a passing `buildVantage` can never satisfy the split-release guard — a build attestation is not production route provenance. self-test 19/19 (6 new cases covering independence from the primary receipt, challenge-handling, and origin identity) · `check-content-capability-slice` self-test unchanged 7/7 · build:check 319/319.
- **S321 (2026-08-19) — Three blockers inherited as fact had all quietly stopped being true, and the crash class that took sign-in down twice is now closed on every leg.** The session opened by re-probing what S320 handed over rather than inheriting it. All three identity blockers were stale: `obeliskgate.com/.well-known/openid-configuration` now answers **200 `application/json`** with a complete OIDC document (recorded as serving HTML), `/login` answers **302** to the provider carrying a full S256 PKCE challenge (recorded as a 503 awaiting a KV quota reset), and `/auth/revoke` answers **401 `invalid_client`** — an implemented endpoint correctly rejecting an unauthenticated client (recorded since D-S302.5 as unshipped). JWKS publishes a key. The whole external identity chain is live, so `real-provider-e2e-pending` — which held production promotion for roughly twenty sessions as "blocked on a sibling repo" — is now **one founder passkey ceremony away**, and that reclassification is backed by `api/provider-chain-readiness.json`, machine-produced by `scripts/verify-provider-chain.mjs` (20/20 self-test) rather than by prose in a JSON field. The receipt deliberately writes no `providerJourney` leg: `verify-provider-journey.mjs` remains their sole writer, because provider routes being reachable is necessary for the journey, not evidence it passed. **The reason nobody noticed is the session's real lesson.** Those blockers were hand-maintained sentences about someone else's service — exactly the claim that goes stale silently — and the gate that should have caught the resulting public lie was `check-public-note-freshness`, which had carried "freshness" in its name for fifteen sessions while asserting only three regexes over voice. It exited 0 the entire time the public status surface told visitors "Sign-in is briefly unavailable" while sign-in worked, because the false claim is plain English and jargon-free. A degradation claim must now be corroborated by a live probe receipt that is present, recent, and actually degraded; the self-test covers both directions, since a gate that punished a *true* outage admission would defeat the honesty CANON-031 requires. **The S319 auth fix turned out to cover one leg of three.** `finishLogin` still issued `RATE_LIMIT.get()`/`.delete()` before its try block and `/api/auth/logout` issued an unguarded `.delete()` — both KV *writes*, so the same free-tier quota exhaustion rejected on both and escaped into Cloudflare 1101. The callback is the costlier leg, since the member has already completed the passkey ceremony by the time they reach it; it now fails closed as a named 503, while logout degrades and reports `storeCleared: false`, because clearing the signed cookie is what actually ends the session and failing the request would leave the credential in the browser. `security-headers-worker.js` also had **no top-level catch across 1,345 lines**, so any escaped throw became a bare 500 with no security headers — what production served twice; a last-resort boundary now logs the route and returns an honest `edge_handler_unavailable` 503, mutation-tested to confirm it does not intercept healthy responses. **Deployed:** Worker to staging (verified on both the zone and `workers.dev` vantages), then production with the release ceremony **8/8**; route provenance was re-probed *after* the deploy so the receipt binds new source to new deployment — **7/7 matched**. Getting there required fixing a false red that had been blocking every production deploy: the ceremony's staging browser gate failed on chromium, firefox and webkit with 30-second timeouts while staging served the homepage in 425ms. Run directly, the test **passes in 35.7s** — a seven-theme axe sweep had outgrown a shared budget. The budget was scoped to the test and not one assertion was relaxed; a timeout is not a readability measurement. **One item was disproven rather than built:** S320 committed to probing `pages.dev` as a corroborating route-provenance vantage, but `pages.dev` returns 404 for `/_health` and `/api/auth/me` and 405 for `OPTIONS /v/rum` — it is the Pages origin *behind* the Worker, so it can never observe Worker routes, and implementing it risked a false `routes-absent-from-deployed-worker` verdict. Re-scoped to the staging `workers_dev` binding, measured this session serving the full 7-route contract.
- **S320 (2026-08-18) — Production is serving current content again, and the instruments that missed the last outage now watch the paths that actually broke.** The static content lane had been unpromoted for 13.8 days: production markup sat at baseline `9527f227` from 2026-08-04 while 839 commits of content — including the homepage Desk module — were built, committed, pushed, and invisible. It is now live. The content lane promoted **259 content-pure paths** onto the deployed tree (`contentLaneHead 60ed3748c`, run 32192776059), withholding 733 repo-internal paths at baseline, and `api/build-sha.json` still reports `baselineSha 9527f227` because the identity backlog genuinely is unpromoted — the honesty that lane was built to preserve. `deploy-currency` moved from a hard FAIL (839 behind · past the 48h CANON-036 ceiling) to `content-current` WARN, and doctor's blocking failure count went 1 → 0. **Getting there exposed a real circular dependency**, not a flaky gate: the Worker deploy required a green doctor, doctor's only blocker was stale production content, and the content lane clears it — but the content lane first blocked on the S317 split-release guard, because nine promoted callers reference `/v/rum`, `/v/desk-reaction` and `/v/desk-presence` and the provenance receipt held no live evidence for them. The routes had been live the whole time; only the evidence was missing, which is exactly the distinction that guard exists to draw. A probe from an unchallenged vantage returned 7/7 matched and the lane opened. **Three observability defects were fixed before the deploy, deliberately, so the deploy was watched by instruments that could see it fail.** `/v/rum` was probed with `OPTIONS` only — a preflight that `corsRumResponse` answers 204 unconditionally, and therefore stayed green through an outage in which `POST` returned 500 for long enough that `data/news-desk-engagement-history.ndjson` never came into existence at all. `/login` was not probed at all, which is why production sign-in returning 500 was found by accident while deploying. Both are now exercised on their real methods against an expected status, and a named `503 auth_store_unavailable` is classified as honest self-restoring degradation while a 500/1101 is judged `down`. The ingest probe sends `synthetic: true`; the Worker validates fully, answers 202, and skips the store write, so the probe cannot pollute the reader-engagement dataset it exists to protect — verified live: `{"ok":true,"synthetic":true}`. **`check-writeback-currency`, the arc's own cut-off detector, was reading green on an unmeasurable window.** It scanned a fixed 60 commits and returned "cannot measure" as `ok: true`, exit 0; once ~60 `[skip ci]` beacon commits accumulate between closeouts the anchor falls out of view and the detector goes permanently, silently green. Reproduced live in both directions inside one session. The window is now derived from the anchor itself, UNMEASURED is a distinct non-pass exit, and automation churn is classified structurally rather than by a subject enumeration that never matched this repo's real cron subjects — 68 false positives down to 6. `deploy-currency` is also now observed at the top of the uptime step instead of below its low-churn short-circuit, because "nothing else changed this hour" is precisely when a staleness clock needs reading.
- **S317 (2026-08-16) — The Desk's reader loop is actually connected, and the numbers behind it are real.** The reaction buttons had been failing with “Signal not delivered. Check your connection” for every visitor. It was never a client bug: the handlers landed 2026-08-10 while the deployed Worker was from 2026-07-31, and `cloudflare/**` is hard-blocked from the content lane that shipped the front end — the lane is architecturally guaranteed to ship the caller and strand the callee. Deployed through the identity lane (which does not release the promotion hold); `/v/desk-reaction` and `/v/desk-presence` now answer 200/204 live. Two honesty defects fell out of it: `worker-route-provenance` was recording the dead routes as `vantage-challenged` while `/_health` returned 200 JSON from the same probe — a clear control now disproves a challenge and `missing` is a first-class state that names the absent routes — and the client no longer blames the visitor's connection for a route we never deployed. **“Lead signal”/“Quiet signal” were not merely unclear, they were wrong**: derived from a hand-set `kind` rather than `day.leadSlug`, so any day with two trending stories printed “Lead signal” on both, while RSS called the same thing “The Quiet Story”. They are now “Today's lead” and “The quiet story”, derived from the real lead, consistent across hub/article/feed, with a legend, and both fields validated. **Per-article statistics now exist**: pageloads, measured engaged time, an attention ratio bridging the 220‑wpm estimate to the measurement, and away-time bands — all server-rendered, all suppressed below their floors. The view counter turned out to already exist (`rum-beacon.js` has been posting per-route to R2 all along), but most rows are `ux` events rather than pageloads: counting rows would have inflated reach roughly 10× and published it as a visitor number. Reader reactions gained the aggregation half they never had (bounded corpus-derived enumeration, never `KV.list`; a shrinking cumulative counter publishes `reset` rather than a fabricated decline), rendered into the existing Director's Report. Founder-reported console errors were all real and all fixed: social icons 404'd on every article (harvested depth-1 chrome pasted into depth-3 pages), and `journey-conductor.js` had 404'd on **every page since S306** because it was predicate-loaded, never hash-named, and therefore unpromotable while the full-site lane is held. Validation: canonical `build:check` **302/302** (up from 295 — `generate-news-pages --check` and `build-news-desk-engagement --check` were real gates that had lived only in an uncalled `news:check` script and had never once run in CI), Playwright desk+compliance 23/23, Worker units 43/43, doctor blockingFailing 0.

- **S316 (2026-08-14) — The public deploy-currency signal told the truth for the first time, and the gate that was supposed to protect it can now see through indirection.** Three defects were masking each other on `/status/`. `check-workflow-git-depth` reported green because its detector matched only a direct `execFileSync('git', ['log'…])` shape and missed `build-deploy-currency.mjs`'s helper-bound `git(['cat-file'…])` calls, so both producing workflows kept running the default depth-1 checkout; in a shallow clone the deployed-sha lookup fails for every non-tip commit, which the producer classified as `diverged` — the most severe state in its vocabulary — against a commit that is an ordinary ancestor of `main`. That false alarm never reached anyone because `status/index.html` read `d.status` while the producer emits `d.state`, so the tile rendered a permanent neutral “Unverified” for all six real states. All three are fixed at the root: the gate now detects history-dependent git through helper bindings and covers `cat-file`/`merge-base`/`describe`; both workflows set `fetch-depth: 0`; the producer refuses to infer divergence from an incomplete clone and publishes `honesty.historyComplete`; and the tile reads the real state with severity that matches it. Re-probed from a full clone, production is **`content-current`** (515 commits behind, shell fingerprints matched) — not diverged. Separately, the E2E compliance gate had been failing *only when production was healthy*, because it asserted “shell fingerprint matched” while the page renders the plural “shell fingerprints matched”. Both publishing crons gained bounded push retry after a transient GitHub 500 silently discarded a full analytics window. An inbound studio-ops propagation was found to have regressed three local-ahead surfaces (the CANON-019 unknown-vs-missing capability distinction, the secrets sibling capability-map fallback, and the startup-brief renderer's evidence + revenue integrations); all were restored locally and shipped upstream as Ark cargo rather than edited in the sibling tree. Validation: canonical `build:check` **295/295 from step one**, Playwright compliance **18/18** including the previously red release-truth test, doctor `blockingFailing: 0`.

- **S315 (2026-08-14) — Public analytics now distinguishes instruments, exposes the whole Studio ecosystem, and gives every Desk illustration its own privacy-safe feedback loop.** Cloudflare GraphQL data is pulled across all 29 active zones into provenance-bearing raw/history receipts and a divided `/stats/ecosystem/` explorer; production, staging, internal, bot, visit, page-load, and edge-request denominators remain separate rather than being blended into one flattering number. `/stats/` now explains why its first-party interaction/performance samples differ from Cloudflare Web Analytics and Dashboard Traffic, with complete-day UTC windows and source dates. Every published Desk article has a Like/Fire/Laugh/Wow panel bound to its generated illustration plus live-reader presence and visible-and-focused reading-time states; low counts are suppressed, sessions are short-lived hashes, and persisted summaries contain no IP, cookie, or visitor identifier. Automation covers nightly Cloudflare pulls, nightly Desk engagement rollups, agent discovery, Lighthouse route tiers, Worker privacy/dedupe behavior, and 56 manually reviewed theme/viewport states. Validation is green: canonical `build:check` passed 295/295 from step one (authoritative receipt: `api/build-check-diagnostics.json`; plan `d808a9610d8b1a4e18defbb4`; source `e71cc9478c5293283a4c0d8e`), alongside 32/32 browser/Axe, 42/42 Worker units, 56/56 manual visual receipts, and 42 News pixel proofs. Worker-backed live reactions/presence remain source-ready but undeployed under the unchanged full-site identity release hold.
- **S312 (2026-08-11) — The Desk proved its light formats with live content, all-theme visual evidence, and a staging content overlay.** A real Roast and a real Signature Bit shipped for 2026-08-11 from primary sources: Cloudflare's Browser Run announcement and KPMG's Q2 AI Pulse report. Both went through the normal News data -> rebuild -> page generation -> stats/feed/claims pipeline, with no fake prediction block and no hand-edited public page. `scripts/check-news-visual-proof.mjs` now verifies the touched News routes across seven themes and desktop/mobile using Chromium visibility checks plus Sharp pixel variance; this was necessary because the local image viewer failed under the Windows sandbox, so visual QA is explicitly machine-inspected rather than falsely claimed as eyeballed. Full `npm run build:check` is green at 295/295 (receipt `cf774febfdc668dae34a51bf`), focused News gates are green, and canonical Hetzner staging was updated through `deploy-staging-content` with 208 overlays and 5 safe removals, identity untouched. Production full-site promotion remains held by the Obelisk real-provider gate; this release uses the identity-isolated content lane.
- **S308 final addendum (2026-08-08) — the desk gained a newsroom, a working track record, and permission to be entertaining.** Three founder challenges each exposed something structurally unbacked. (1) *"Do we have an editor or reviewer or publisher persona?"* — no, all six were commentators. Answering it surfaced a P0: `buildLedgerFromDays()` passed only `predictions`, never `resolutions`, so grading was erased on every rebuild and the "publicly graded" claim on every page could not work. Fixed with a committed resolutions source, deterministic re-attachment, validation at the rebuild boundary, and a `--resolve` path that refuses a grade without an evidence URL. Added THE EDITOR / STANDARDS / CORRECTIONS as roles distinct from commentators; `runStandards()` blocks a figure asserted in commentary that appears in no cited fact, and `editorialReview()` is the refusal mechanism that makes safe autonomy reachable. (2) *"Why is the grade about a year later?"* — every published prediction landed 326–510 days out: individually falsifiable, collectively unfalsifiable on any timescale a reader checks. Standards now blocks an all-long-horizon story and the drafter proposes a 45/120/240-day ladder; published dates stand as written rather than being re-dated to flatter the record. (3) *"Only long-graded predictions feels very robotic"* — correct, and structural: every story required a dated prediction, so a sharp line about a viral moment could not publish without bolting a forecast onto the joke, while the radar filtered spectacle out as uncastable. Six formats now carry different bars, each persona owns a recurring column, and a `spectacle` beat makes viral material findable. The one rule that never relaxes is that a stated fact must be real and cited in every format. Self-tests: news-desk 52 → 114, drafter 30 → 45, trends 56, disclosure 17, public-safe 20.

- **S310 (2026-08-11) — The Desk's numbers are computed, and readers can answer back.** Every published figure is derived by `lib/news-stats.mjs` into a byte-checked `api/news-desk-stats.json` the renderer reads, so the page and the feed cannot drift apart; accuracy withholds a percentage until four calls are graded, and sources count unique publishers rather than citations. The canned "The desk disagrees" line is retired on the founder's flag — true on two stories, meaningless on the three with a single voice — replaced by an axis plotting each voice's actual position. Reader reactions are live as UI (editorial prompts plus a per-voice vote feeding ORSON's report), identity-free, counts shown only when the server returns them. Each persona's prose now uses its own visual register. A founder-reported crude cartoon was fixed. Reaction COUNTS remain dark: the endpoint ships in the Worker, whose promotion gate holds on `real-provider-e2e-pending` — and the Ark inbox revealed the deeper truth that this site has no Obelisk sign-in control at all and is one of 0/43 relying parties still on v1, so that gate cannot clear until the Passport v2 migration the founder has scheduled as its own session.

- **S309 (2026-08-10) — The Desk has a Director, and the repo can repair its own derived graph.** `/news/directors-report/` is live: ORSON assigns the stories and then publishes a review ranking all seven writers, with something to work on for every one of them including rank 1, and the blame taken where it is his. Performance (assignments · words · panels · leads · graded calls) is DERIVED from the corpus by `deriveDeskPerformance()`; only the ranking and the feedback are authored, because a review generated from a template would be pretending to be judgement. It shipped with the generic site share card — the generator hardcoded it and the auto-promoter skips news pages, since it reads an `og:title` the Desk never emits — and the first fix rendered a technically valid card that said "THE DISPATCH · No account required · double opt-in" over a performance review with the headline clipped to the opposite of its meaning; only looking at the pixels caught it. Now fixed at the generator, with the card's footer naming ORSON as an AI editor so the disclosure reaches feeds ahead of the page. `scripts/resync-derived.mjs` rebuilds the transitive closure of the evidence graph in topological order after a rebase, because a conflict list shows collisions rather than dependents; it is registered in build:check and proven in production. It covers 17 graph nodes, not every derived artifact, and is silent on the rest — a limit recorded rather than papered over.

- **S308 answered the founder's editorial-engine directive by fixing the structure underneath it, not by adding pundits — and shipped The Desk's first working newsletter.** Last updated: 2026-08-08. The cast was one-dimensional *by construction*: `direction` was a single scalar and `computeHeat` was mean pairwise distance on it, so REX/MARA/DOT were three points on one hype line and every debate had the same shape. S308 added a second axis (`horizon`, immediate ↔ structural) so "we agree it's big, we disagree on when" — the most common real technology disagreement — became expressible, and `heatBreakdown()` now names the *shape* of a split (`split-on-worth` / `split-on-timing` / `split-on-both`). Backward compatibility is structural, not incidental: `horizon` defaults to 0 and the divisor stays at the 1-D maximum, so the published 2026-08-07 heat values are provably unmoved (asserted by test, and confirmed by a byte-stable ledger/carousel/feed under `--check`). Three new personas were added by *epistemic role* rather than optimism — VERA (has run it in production), ECHO (has seen the cycle before), JUNO (tracks who it lands on) — and `castForStory()` seats a beat-owning anchor plus its declared rival, so six voices produce variety by rotation instead of noise. REX/MARA/DOT were deliberately RETAINED: the prediction ledger is hash-chained and references their ids, so retiring one would orphan the verifiable public track record that is the product's entire claim. `personaForm()` turns that ledger into a writing directive — a persona on a cold streak is written chastened, on a hot streak emboldened — gated so that below four resolved calls the standing is `unproven` and carries no tone shift at all. Cadence became structurally possible: `EDITIONS` (Wire 06:00 · Midday · Close · Late Night) moves the volume cap from per-day to per-edition, and a half-editioned day is rejected. `scripts/news-trend-radar.mjs` + `scripts/lib/news-trends.mjs` (56 self-tests) sweep free, key-less sources into corroborated topics scored on corroboration > engagement, with hard disqualifications for single-source rumour, already-covered re-runs, uncastable beats, and vendor marketing. Two real bugs were found by running it live rather than by reading it: every Google News link is a `news.google.com` redirect, so 100 independent outlets were collapsing to one domain and corroboration — the highest-weighted signal — was silently dead; and lab blogs publish customer case studies that scored as news. Both root-fixed with regression tests; the live queue went 7 → 24 with genuine multi-source corroboration. **The Dispatch is live**: Brevo list 3 + double-opt-in template 1, `supabase/functions/subscribe-desk-dispatch` deployed (`verify_jwt=false`, pinned in `config.toml`), deliberately identity-free because `send-member-newsletter` is Vault-Member-gated and The Desk requires no account. Verified against the live endpoint 5/5 including negative controls (malformed rejected, foreign origin 403, real double-opt-in dispatched); the contact correctly stayed OFF list 3 pending confirmation, which is the consent contract working. CANON-053 caught a blocking light-theme defect the source review missed — a flat `background:var(--gold)` Subscribe button rendered near-black-on-dark-amber because light-theme `--gold` (#7a5c00) is a *text* token; fixed by reusing the sitewide `.button` gradient so contrast stays one design-system decision. Also root-fixed a pre-existing genius-list self-contradiction (the generator marked BRAND items actionable while writing "requires founder sign-off" into their rationale). `npm run build:check` all steps EXIT 0; news-desk self-test 25 → 52; visual QA 42 hash-bound captures, blockingDefectsOpen 0. The Obelisk staging-callback release hold is untouched and remains the only blocker.
